Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Clang Associations: Speech governed by sounds, such as rhyming, rather than the meaning of words.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Derailment or Loose Associations: A speech pattern of switching rapidly from one topic to another with no logical connection between thoughts.\n  - Tangentiality: Speech that deviates entirely from the question and fails to answer directly.\n  - Incoherence or Word Salad: Severe fragmentation of speech and sentence structure, making it nearly impossible to understand.\n  - Neologisms: Using made-up words that have meaning only to the individual.\n  - Clang Associations: Speech governed by sounds, such as rhyming, rather than the meaning of words.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

So, most of that early period was just the voices and paranoia without the depression.\n\nDoctor: During the two months you've been experiencing depressive symptoms, have you had any periods where your mood improved significantly, or has the depression been consistently severe?\n\nPatient: No, my mood hasn't improved at all over the past two months. I have felt constantly sad and depressed every single day without any breaks. It has just been a continuous, heavy weight the entire time.\n\nDoctor: For the six months before the depression started, did you experience any notable impairment in social or occupational functioning solely due to the voices and feeling of being watched, or was it relatively manageable?\n\nPatient: No, it wasn't manageable at all and caused a lot of problems. The voices and the constant feeling of being watched made me lose all motivation to do my daily tasks, and I completely withdrew from my friends and social life during those six months.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any times during the past eight months when you had a period of at least two weeks where you didn't experience any depressive symptoms, but still had the voices and feeling of being watched, and if so, how did you function during those times?\n\nPatient: Yes, during the first six months, I had the voices and paranoia without any depression for weeks at a time. I was terrified and struggling to cope with those fears, but I didn't have the heavy sadness, lack of motivation, or sleep issues that began recently."

========== INPUT [patient] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ psychotic_symptoms\n  - Derailment or Loose Associations: A speech pattern of switching rapidly from one topic to another with no logical connection between thoughts.\n  - Tangentiality: Speech that deviates entirely from the question and fails to answer directly.\n  - Incoherence or Word Salad: Severe fragmentation of speech and sentence structure, making it nearly impossible to understand.\n  - Neologisms: Using made-up words that have meaning only to the individual.\n  - Clang Associations: Speech governed by sounds, such as rhyming, rather than the meaning of words.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  - Derailment or Loose Associations: A speech pattern of switching rapidly from one topic to another with no logical connection between thoughts.\n  - Tangentiality: Speech that deviates entirely from the question and fails to answer directly.\n  - Incoherence or Word Salad: Severe fragmentation of speech and sentence structure, making it nearly impossible to understand.\n  - Neologisms: Using made-up words that have meaning only to the individual.\n  - Clang Associations: Speech governed by sounds, such as rhyming, rather than the meaning of words.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Emotional Numbness: A state where emotions feel dulled or paralyzed.\n  - Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Hypersomnia: Sleeping for long periods at night or excessive sleep during the day.\n  - Nightmare Disorder: Sleep disturbance caused by repeated and distressing dreams.\n  - Non-Restorative Sleep: Feeling unrefreshed despite having slept enough.\n  Duration: 2 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - It was only about two months ago that the heavy depression and emptiness set in. I was terrified and struggling to cope with those fears, but I didn't have the heavy sadness, lack of motivation, or sleep issues that began recently.\n\nDoctor: During those periods where you had the voices and feeling of being watched without depressive symptoms, were you able to maintain any semblance of normal life, such as working, socializing, or taking care of yourself, or was your functioning still significantly impaired?"

Clang Associations: Speech governed by sounds, such as rhyming, rather than the meaning of words.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 8 months\n▸ depressive_episode\n  - Depressed Mood: A persistently sunken mood or feeling of depression.\n  - Tearfulness: A state of frequent crying or inability to hold back tears.\n  - Sadness: A state where a sad or gloomy mood is maintained continuously.\n  - Emptiness: A feeling of inner emptiness or a state of feeling no emotion.\n  - Initial Insomnia: Difficulty falling asleep and taking a long time to do so.\n  - Middle Insomnia: Waking up frequently during sleep or having difficulty falling back asleep.\n  - Early Morning Awakening: Waking up too early and being unable to fall back asleep.\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - I was terrified and struggling to cope with those fears, but I didn't have the heavy sadness, lack of motivation, or sleep issues that began recently.\n\nDoctor: During those periods where you had the voices and feeling of being watched without depressive symptoms, were you able to maintain any semblance of normal life, such as working, socializing, or taking care of yourself, or was your functioning still significantly impaired?\n\nPatient: Even without the depression, my functioning was still highly impaired and far from normal. I struggled significantly with basic self-care and daily tasks because I had a severe lack of motivation to do anything. On top of that, I completely avoided interacting with others or socializing because I had absolutely no interest in doing so.\n\nDoctor: So, to confirm, you've had continuous psychotic symptoms for eight months, with a significant portion of that time, about six months, spent with psychotic symptoms but without depressive symptoms, and the last two months with both psychotic and depressive symptoms; is that a correct summary of your experience?"

Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.
